Disassembly: dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.bin
=====================================

Source: commercial dryer control board, Model PCT, firmware v4.20 - a hot
        air resin dryer (industrial plastics-processing equipment, not a
        laundry appliance). See dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.summary.md for chip/
        content identification, dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.protocol.md for the
        SPI CCP serial protocol this device implements, and
        dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.messages.md for the full display-message table.

Architecture: Intel MCS-51 (8051-family, likely 8052 variant - see summary
              for the Timer 2 vector evidence)

Range disassembled: $0000-$F28B (the used region - the file's remaining
                     ~5% is unprogrammed $FF fill, not disassembled here)

Method: linear-sweep disassembly (decodes every byte in address order as
        an instruction, does not trace actual code flow). This is a real,
        known limitation, not an oversight: any embedded data mixed in
        with code - jump/lookup tables, the display-message table, other
        binary tables - will be misdecoded as bogus instructions wherever
        it appears. Treat runs of nonsensical output as probable data
        regions, not as evidence the tool is broken. A real reverse-
        engineering pass would need a recursive/flow-tracing disassembler
        (follows actual JMP/CALL/branch targets, only decodes what's
        truly reachable) to separate code from data reliably - not
        attempted here.

Format: <address>  <raw bytes>  <mnemonic + operands>  [; inline comment]
        Bit addresses shown as byte.bit (e.g. $20.3); direct addresses
        $80 and above shown by SFR name where recognized (P0/P1/P2/P3,
        SP, DPL/DPH, TCON/TMOD/TL0/TH0/TL1/TH1, SCON/SBUF, IE/IP, PSW,
        ACC, B, and the 8052 Timer 2 registers), otherwise as $xx.

External memory map (identified so far):
        $C000-$C7FF  M48T02-70 TIMEKEEPER NVRAM (2KB, battery-backed).
                     Control+clock registers at $C7F8-$C7FF, confirmed
                     directly against the chip's real datasheet (user-
                     supplied) - see the $5728/$57B0 routines below.
        $A000-$A7FF  Volatile working RAM (confirmed: zeroed by the
                     startup clear loop at $00A9, and separately
                     $A481-$A775 by the loop at $0037) - NOT the NVRAM,
                     despite being a same-size, similarly-based region.
                     Also holds the SPI CCP response-staging buffer
                     (~$A67B onward) and per-response field variables
                     (ADD at $C0CB [NVRAM-mapped, not $A0xx - a config
                     value], CMD1/CMD2/RES at $A6C3-$A6C5, ERR at $A6D3).

Code-space (MOVC-addressed) data tables identified so far:
        $E460-$EB28  Display message table - 193 entries, 9 bytes each
                     (8-char field + $00). Full catalog in
                     dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.messages.md. Looked up via
                     'index*9 + base' at 15+ call sites throughout the
                     code (compiler-generated pattern, not one shared
                     routine) - some sites hardcode a pre-offset base
                     (e.g. $EA51 = index 169) rather than always
                     computing from $E460, which looked like a second
                     table until the math was checked.
        $E2B9+       Structurally different (non-text) table, same
                     9-byte-record shape but binary fields, not
                     characters - hypothesized as per-parameter min/max/
                     type metadata paired with the message table above;
                     not confirmed.
        $E451+       3-byte-record table (handler+match-byte shape, like
                     $0A28's dispatch tables) - likely another command/
                     state dispatch table, not text.

Comments: added ONLY where actually traced and evidenced by reading the
        code, or (for the NVRAM chip identity and the SPI CCP protocol)
        supplied directly by the user from the real datasheet/spec and
        then CRC/byte verified against the code - not sprinkled across
        the whole file. Boxed banner comments (====) mark identified
        functional sections; dashed banners (----) mark identified
        utility/shared routines or related-but-less-certain code; short
        inline comments (after the instruction) mark specific noteworthy
        lines within an otherwise-uncommented stretch. The large majority
        of this file is still bare, unannotated disassembly - most of it
        hasn't been traced yet. Identified so far:
          - Interrupt vector table ($0000-$002D) and reset/startup code
            ($002E onward): stack init, RAM/external-memory clear loops,
            a sequence of table-driven peripheral init calls
          - $00C7, $00D0, $00EE: shared ROM-table-unpacking utilities
            (generic - which table depends on the caller, not identified
            here)
          - $0148: Serial (UART) interrupt - TI/RI flag handling
          - $0199: External INT0 handler - keypad/button-panel read via a
            bit-serial shift register (P1.5 data-in, P1.6 clock, P1.7
            data-out/daisy-chain out)
          - $0A28: command/state dispatch table search (return-address-
            as-table-pointer trick) - the mechanism behind the SPI CCP
            per-zone command tables in dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.protocol.md
          - $450F, $645F-$64C9: UART init and TX/RX primitives
          - $5728, $57B0, $57F6, $5827: M48T02 TIMEKEEPER NVRAM read/
            write/reset routines (see external memory map above)
          - $63CE/$63D9/$63E9: SPI CCP transmit staging buffer primitives
            (reset index / queue byte)
          - $6456: SPI CCP transmit routine (RS-485 enable + SBUF loop)
          - $65B6: SPI CCP response HEADER BUILDER - confirmed field-for-
            field against the real SPI CCP V4.00 spec and CRC-verified
            against live protocol captures (DLE,SOH,DEVID,ADD,CMD1,CMD2,
            RES,ERR,DLE,STX) - see dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.protocol.md
          - $65FB: closes a response frame (DLE,ETX + CRC calc/append
            via $6453, not traced in full)
          - $E45F-$EB28: the display message table itself (data, not
            code - see dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.messages.md)

=====================================

      ; ----------------------------------------------------------------------
      ; UTILITY: compare current table pointer (R6:DPH) against a saved end
      ; value - used by callers of the unpack routines below as a loop-end
      ; test. Not display/keypad-specific; shared plumbing.

      ; ----------------------------------------------------------------------
      ; UTILITY: unpack a 4-byte-header ROM table into internal RAM.
      ; Header (4 bytes, read via MOVC): start-offset, end-offset, table
      ; pointer hi, table pointer lo. Copies bytes from the table into
      ; internal RAM starting at R0 until R0 reaches R1 (the header's
      ; start/end values). Generic - used to load whatever constant data a
      ; caller needs (segment tables, message tables, etc.) - which table
      ; depends on the caller, not on this routine itself.
      ; ----------------------------------------------------------------------
00d0  e4        CLR A
00d1  93        MOVC A,@A+DPTR

      ; EXTERNAL INT0 HANDLER - keypad/button-panel shift-register read

      ; ----------------------------------------------------------------------
      ; Sub-handler: BIT-SERIAL SHIFT-IN of one button/switch-panel byte.
      ;   P1.5 = data-in   P1.6 = clock strobe   P1.7 = data-out
      ; Each interrupt call shifts one bit from P1.5 into a byte being
      ; assembled at $3F (via RLC), counting bits in $3E. After 8 bits,
      ; the finished byte is saved to $40 - almost certainly the current
      ; keypad/button-panel state byte. P1.6 is then pulsed (clock strobe)
      ; and the bit is echoed back out on P1.7, consistent with reading a
      ; bank of physical buttons through a daisy-chained shift-register
      ; chip (e.g. 74HC165-style) instead of one MCU pin per button.
      ; ----------------------------------------------------------------------
01ed  a2 95     MOV C,P1.5
01ef  e5 3f     MOV A,$3f
01f1  33        RLC A
01f2  f5 3f     MOV $3f,A
01f4  05 3e     INC$3e
01f6  e5 3e     MOV A,$3e
01f8  b4 08 09  CJNE A,#$08,$0204
01fb  c2 14     CLR$22.4
01fd  c2 13     CLR$22.3
01ff  c2 12     CLR$22.2

      ; 8 bits collected - save assembled byte to $40 (button-panel state) and clear state flags
0201  85 3f 40  MOV $40,$3f

      ; ======================================================================
      ; COMMAND DISPATCH TABLE SEARCH (linear scan, return-address trick).
      ; Called via LCALL immediately followed by INLINE TABLE DATA (not
      ; code) - this routine POPs its own return address off the stack to
      ; use as the table pointer, so each call site's own literal table
      ; sits right after the LCALL instruction. Entry format: 3 bytes
      ; [handler_lo][handler_hi][match_byte] repeated, terminated by a
      ; $00,$00 sentinel (a wildcard/default entry). Searches for a match
      ; against R7 (typically CMD2), then does an indirect return into
      ; the matched entry's handler address - never returns to the
      ; instruction after its own inline table, always jumps into a
      ; handler instead. See the SPI CCP command tables this uncovered
      ; (dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.protocol.md's zone-command answer).
      ; ======================================================================

      ; ======================================================================
      ; NVRAM (M48T02-70 TIMEKEEPER) - READ routine
      ; Chip confirmed at base $C000 (control+clock registers $C7F8-$C7FF,
      ; matching the M48T02 datasheet's 7F8-7FF layout exactly - identified
      ; from a user-supplied datasheet excerpt, not guessed).
      ;   ORL $C7F8,#$40  - sets bit 6 (the datasheet's "seventh bit",
      ;                     0-indexed) = READ bit -> halts TIMEKEEPER
      ;                     register updates so a stable value can be read
      ;   ...reads $C7FB (hours) and $C7FA (minutes) into RAM ($A19A/$A19B)...
      ;   ANL $C7F8,#$bf  - clears the READ bit -> resumes updating
      ; ======================================================================

      ; ======================================================================
      ; NVRAM (M48T02-70 TIMEKEEPER) - WRITE/RESET routine
      ;   ORL $C7F8,#$80  - sets bit 7 ("eighth bit") = WRITE bit -> halts
      ;                     updates so new values can be loaded
      ;   ...writes hours/minutes from RAM, seconds=0, date=month=year=1...
      ;   ANL $C7F8,#$7f  - clears the WRITE bit -> "transfers the values
      ;                     of all time registers to the actual TIMEKEEPER
      ;                     counters" (datasheet's own wording)
      ; Writing date/month/year=1 rather than real values suggests this is
      ; a reset-to-default path (e.g. after a battery failure), not a
      ; general user-facing clock-set routine.
      ; ======================================================================

57b3  90 c7 f8  MOV DPTR,#$c7f8
57b6  e0        MOVX A,@DPTR
57b7  44 80     ORL A,#$80
57b9  f0        MOVX @DPTR,A

      ; ======================================================================
      ; SPI CCP: RESPONSE HEADER BUILDER - confirmed against the real SPI
      ; CCP V4.00 spec (Society of the Plastics Industry, Committee on
      ; Communication Protocol) and CRC-verified against live captures -
      ; see dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.protocol.md. Builds exactly:
      ;   DLE,SOH,DEVID,ADD,CMD1,CMD2,RES,ERR,DLE,STX  (10 bytes)
      ;   DEVID=$22 is a fixed immediate; ADD/CMD1/CMD2/RES/ERR are read
      ;   from RAM variables ($C0CB/$A6C3/$A6C4/$A6C5/$A6D3) - CMD2 at
      ;   $A6C4 is the just-received command being echoed back.
